Oh... um... well... I know I shouldn't think this way, and I really don't want to upset you or make you think I'm, you know, a danger to myself or anything. I try to stay positive, I really do.

But... sometimes, especially late at night when it's quiet... I catch myself thinking that maybe my husband might just be better off without me. Like, without all this burden I'm causing with the mortgage and everything. 

I haven't made a plan or done anything like that, I promise! I would never want to actually do something to hurt him. It's just... the thought keeps coming back, and it honestly scares me a little bit. I know it's a terrible thing to think, and I feel so guilty for even letting it cross my mind. 

I hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. Is it... is it normal for people to have thoughts like that when they're feeling this overwhelmed? I just want to be honest so you can help me fix it...

Um... well... I don't know if I'd say "unusually" high-spirited, because I really try to stay positive and energetic for my team, you know? I want to be a good influence. But... I always apologized right away, of course. I hate being that kind of person... I really don't want you to think I'm normally like that. I just... I had so much energy, it almost felt like it was spilling over into impatience. Does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you're asking about?

I mean, everyone has days where they feel a little gloomy for no reason... is that something you hear a lot?\n\nDoctor: Has it felt like you don’t get any pleasure or satisfaction out of things you used to enjoy, like hiking, yoga, or taking photos?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess that's... that's exactly it. It's just... \n\nWhen I look at my camera, or think about going out to the garden, I just feel so... empty about it? There's no spark anymore. I mean, everyone has days where they feel a little gloomy for no reason... is that something you hear a lot?\n\nDoctor: Has it felt like you don’t get any pleasure or satisfaction out of things you used to enjoy, like hiking, yoga, or taking photos?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess that's... that's exactly it. It's like I mentioned before, with the hiking and the yoga and the photos. I really do want to be the kind of person who enjoys those things, I promise I do. It's just... \n\nWhen I look at my camera, or think about going out to the garden, I just feel so... empty about it? There's no spark anymore. It just feels like another chore on the to-do list, and I'm already so tired. I know I shouldn't be letting it all go, and I feel awful about canceling on my friends so much. \n\nI'm trying not to be lazy about it, I really am. It's just hard to force yourself to do things when there's no... no joy at the end of it, you know? Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too negative.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant loss of energy or feel that everything takes extra effort, even when you're not physically tired?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes. Actually, that's kind of a big part of why I finally decided to come in. I hate to admit it, because I really try to just push through and be productive, but... I just feel worn out all the time lately. \n\nIt's like, even after I spend a full night in bed, I wake up and my body just feels like it's made of sandbags. I try so hard to keep up with everything, but just getting through a normal workday, or even doing something small like folding the laundry... it just takes absolutely everything I have right now. \n\nI feel terrible that I can't just handle my normal responsibilities without feeling this way. Is that... does that sound like something you see a lot? I really hope I don't just sound like I'm making excuses for being tired...\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately, like eating less than usual or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I know sometimes people stop eating when they feel down, but I've always been pretty good about making sure I have my meals. I don't want to cause any other health problems, you know? \n\nI think my weight has stayed pretty much the same. I try to make healthy choices when I can, even when I'm feeling so tired. I haven't really noticed my clothes fitting differently or anything like that. \n\nI hope that's okay? Like, is it normal to feel this way but still be eating normally? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information...\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have been slower than usual, or that it's harder to make decisions, even about small things?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I have noticed a little bit of that. I really try not to let it affect my work, I promise. I'm usually very sharp, but lately... \n\nIt's just, sometimes at work, I'll be looking at some survey results or reading a client email, and I'll read it like three times and realize I haven't actually absorbed a single word. And making choices, even silly little ones... like, just deciding which spreadsheet I should open first to start my day can feel just... weirdly impossible? \n\nI feel so bad about it, because I know my team relies on me to be on top of things. I'm trying really hard to stay focused and not let anything slip through the cracks. Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound like I'm just being incompetent at my job... is it normal for things to feel a bit foggy like that?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not good enough, or been blaming yourself for things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I have been struggling a little bit with that. I try really hard not to be too hard on myself, because I know it doesn't help anything, but... \n\nLately, I just keep replaying this decision we made about our mortgage. I keep telling myself I was just... so selfish and reckless. I feel like I put our whole household at risk. I mean, my husband is so sweet, he keeps reminding me that we made the decision together and that it's fine, but... I just can't shake this feeling that I messed everything up. \n\nI feel awful even complaining about it, because I know I have a good life and a supportive husband. I shouldn't be dwelling on things like this, right? I just... I hope I don't sound ungrateful or like I'm just throwing a pity party. Is it common to get stuck on feeling like you've ruined things, even when people tell you you haven't?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself thinking about death or feeling like life isn't worth living lately?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well... I know I shouldn't think this way, and I really don't want to upset you or make you think I'm, you know, a danger to myself or anything. I try to stay positive, I really do.\n\nBut... sometimes, especially late at night when it's quiet... I catch myself thinking that maybe my husband might just be better off without me. Like, without all this burden I'm causing with the mortgage and everything. \n\nI haven't made a plan or done anything like that, I promise! I would never want to actually do something to hurt him. It's just... the thought keeps coming back, and it honestly scares me a little bit. I know it's a terrible thing to think, and I feel so guilty for even letting it cross my mind. \n\nI hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. Is it... is it normal for people to have thoughts like that when they're feeling this overwhelmed? I just want to be honest so you can help me fix it...\n\nDoctor: Have you had any previous episodes where you felt this way for a period of time, or is this the first time you've experienced these symptoms?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm trying to think. I really want to give you the right answer, but... Like everything is just kind of gray. \n\nI know I shouldn't think that way, and I try really hard to snap out of it. It's probably just a phase, right? I mean, everyone has days where they feel a little gloomy for no reason... is that something you hear a lot?\n\nDoctor: Has it felt like you don’t get any pleasure or satisfaction out of things you used to enjoy, like hiking, yoga, or taking photos?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess that's... that's exactly it. It's just... \n\nWhen I look at my camera, or think about going out to the garden, I just feel so... empty about it? There's no spark anymore. It's just hard to force yourself to do things when there's no... no joy at the end of it, you know? Does that make sense? I just feel worn out all the time lately. \n\nIt's like, even after I spend a full night in bed, I wake up and my body just feels like it's made of sandbags. I try so hard to keep up with everything, but just getting through a normal workday, or even doing something small like folding the laundry... it just takes absolutely everything I have right now. \n\nI feel terrible that I can't just handle my normal responsibilities without feeling this way. I mean, I know sometimes people stop eating when they feel down, but I've always been pretty good about making sure I have my meals. I don't want to cause any other health problems, you know? \n\nI think my weight has stayed pretty much the same. I try to make healthy choices when I can, even when I'm feeling so tired. I haven't really noticed my clothes fitting differently or anything like that. \n\nI hope that's okay? Like, is it normal to feel this way but still be eating normally?   "note": "The patient endorsed a current major depressive episode lasting more than two months and reported a previous episode lasting at least two weeks, meeting criteria for a recurrent episode. Additionally, the patient described a distinct period of abnormally elevated energy, irritability, and behavioral changes following the mortgage signing, which may represent a manic or hypomanic episode. This new information shifts the diagnosis from a recurrent major depressive disorder (F33.2) to a bipolar disorder (F31.3-F31.9), but since the focus remains on the depressive episode, the most accurate code remains F33.3 (Major depressive disorder, recurrent, severe without psychotic features).",
